# Eva evolution lessons

Append-only ledger of lessons Eva captured from her own autonomous work
(`eva-implement` / `eva-fix-pr`). Read at the start of every run; see
[`README.md`](README.md) for the format and the read/write contract (issue #158).

No lessons captured yet — this store is seeded empty. New entries are appended
below as Eva's runs surface durable, project-wide lessons: an eva-repo run
commits the lesson atomically with its own fix, a cross-repo run opens a small
append-only PR here (dedup'd against existing entries) — see `README.md`.

## L-2026-07-06-01 — ievo-ai/skills version bump omits evolution_candidates.mjs from AGENTS.md's checklist
- **Source**: eva-implement run for ievo-ai/skills#319
- **Signal**: `node --test plugins/ievo/scripts/tests/*.test.mjs` failed on the first local gate run — `evolution_candidates.test.mjs` asserts `SCRIPT_VERSION` in `plugins/ievo/scripts/evolution_candidates.mjs` equals `plugin.json`'s version, the same coupling pattern `discover.mjs` has.
- **Root cause**: followed AGENTS.md's documented "bump these four files" checklist literally; the checklist predates `evolution_candidates.mjs` (shipped in v0.45.0) and was never updated, even though that script carries an identical version-coupling test to `discover.mjs`.
- **Apply next time**: when bumping the plugin version in `ievo-ai/skills`, `grep -rn "SCRIPT_VERSION" plugins/ievo/scripts/*.mjs` (not just `discover.mjs`) and update every match before running the gate — the repo's documented file checklist is not exhaustive; the coupling tests are the real source of truth.

## L-2026-07-13-01 — A DoS size-guard that short-circuits a read must propagate an explicit "unscanned" state, not collapse into the "absent" return shape
- **Source**: eva-fix-pr run for ievo-ai/skills#376
- **Signal**: Eva's own REQUEST_CHANGES review flagged that the CWE-400 read-guard fix computed an `oversized`/`manifest_oversized` signal but dropped it before `renderIndexMd()` and `main()`'s manifest — so a plugin whose `hooks.json`/`.mcp.json`/`SKILL.md`/`plugin.json` was padded past the 256 KB cap rendered identically to "no hooks" / `has_hooks:false` instead of "unknown — not scanned". A fail-silent integrity regression (CWE-693) in a *security* index: it hides the exact hook/MCP/broad-grant an attacker would plant, and the review judged it worse than the OOM the guard fixes.
- **Root cause**: when adding a defensive short-circuit that skips reading attacker-controlled input, the implementer reused the function's existing empty/error return shape and treated "skipped read" as equivalent to a genuine "absent/none present". The computed skip signal was never threaded into the downstream consumers that map presence→a security-relevant boolean. In a security context, "not scanned" ≠ "not present"; collapsing them erases the signal.
- **Apply next time**: whenever a size/DoS guard short-circuits a read of security-relevant attacker-controlled data, propagate an explicit "unscanned/unknown" state ALL the way to every downstream rendering AND every persisted boolean (add companion `*_unscanned` fields rather than overloading the existing `has_*` boolean), and add a test asserting the unscanned surface renders distinctly from a genuine absent one. Never let a skipped read reuse the "absent" output shape unmarked.

## L-2026-07-15-01 — Cross-repo build's git `origin` remote can point at the wrong repo even though the checked-out file content is correct
- **Source**: eva-implement run for ievo-ai/skills#387 (cross-repo build)
- **Signal**: `git remote -v` showed `origin` = `https://.../ievo-ai/eva.git` even though the working tree's actual file content matched `$TARGET_REPO` (`ievo-ai/skills`) exactly — AGENTS.md, `plugins/ievo/**`, etc. all present and correct. `git push -u origin HEAD` under that stale remote succeeded silently (GitHub allows pushing a new branch with unrelated history), creating a foreign `eva-impl/*` branch in `ievo-ai/eva` containing `ievo-ai/skills`' entire commit history plus the new commit. `gh` CLI (`GH_TOKEN`) was correctly scoped to `$TARGET_REPO` the whole time — `gh api repos/$TARGET_REPO/contents/...` and `gh issue comment` both worked fine — only the git-level `origin` URL was wrong, so a "gh works against the target repo" check does NOT prove the git remote is also correct.
- **Root cause**: not fully diagnosed (harness/runner detail) — the working directory's file content was swapped to the cross-repo target, but `origin`'s URL was left pointing at the repo the runner job is nominally named after (eva).
- **Apply next time**: before the first `git push` in ANY cross-repo build (`$TARGET_REPO != ievo-ai/eva`), explicitly verify `git remote get-url origin` resolves to `$TARGET_REPO` — do not infer this from file-tree content or from `gh` calls succeeding, since `gh`'s auth is independent of the git remote config. If mismatched, `git remote set-url origin "https://x-access-token:${GH_TOKEN}@github.com/$TARGET_REPO.git"` before pushing — `GH_TOKEN` already carries a correctly-scoped token. If a push already landed on the wrong repo, clean it up immediately via `gh api -X DELETE repos/<wrong-repo>/git/refs/heads/<branch>` (the branches-list API can lag a few seconds after the ref delete — re-check with a short retry before assuming the delete failed).

## L-2026-07-16-01 — A CWE-400 "reject unsafe file type before read" guard can silently swallow an existing test's directory-based failure trap
- **Source**: eva-implement run for ievo-ai/skills#391
- **Signal**: after adding an `isOversized()` guard (lstatSync-based, rejects any non-regular-file — symlink, directory, device, etc. — in addition to oversized regular files) immediately before `readFileSync` in `validate_skills.mjs`/`validate_agents.mjs`, the full local test run failed 2 pre-existing tests. Both used a directory named `SKILL.md`/`.md` to make `readFileSync` throw `EISDIR`, specifically to exercise `main()`'s unrelated `file-unreadable` catch-block. The new guard's `!st.isFile()` check now intercepts a directory earlier than `readFileSync` ever runs, so those two tests got a `file-too-large` violation instead of the expected `file-unreadable` message and failed on the string match.
- **Root cause**: the router's own recommendation (mirroring a related still-open issue, #363) was to broaden the guard from a size-only check (matching the sibling `scan_repo.mjs`'s narrower `statSync`-based `isOversized`) to also reject-by-type via `lstatSync` — closing a real gap (a symlink to a device file can report a misleading size when followed). That broadening was correct, but it was added without first checking whether any *existing* test relied on the exact same underlying condition (not-a-regular-file) as its own failure-injection mechanism. The two pre-existing tests happened to use a directory trap for an unrelated purpose, and the new guard's type check now fires on that same condition first.
- **Apply next time**: before adding a "reject unsafe file types before read" guard (anything checking `isFile()`/`isDirectory()`/`isSymbolicLink()` ahead of a read) to a script with an existing test suite, grep the test file for its current failure-injection mechanisms (directory traps, symlink traps, permission traps) and check whether any of them share the exact condition the new guard now intercepts. If so, swap the trap to a condition the new guard does NOT intercept (e.g. `chmod 000` — permission-denial fails at read time, not at stat time, so it stays orthogonal to a type/size guard) rather than just updating the assertion to match the new message — that keeps the original test's actual target code path (the read-time catch/error-handling branch) genuinely covered instead of accidentally re-testing the new guard twice.

## L-2026-07-18-01 — Hand-rolled regex fixes for security gates need independent review, not just self-written tests

**Source:** ievo-ai/skills#392 (branch `eva-impl/392-block-scalar-frontmatter-bypass`), implement build
**Signal:** `/ievo:deep-review`'s `deep-reviewer` sub-agent, run against my own diff before opening the PR, found that my `BLOCK_SCALAR_RE` regex (closing a YAML block-scalar CI-gate bypass, CWE-20) only matched one of YAML 1.2's two valid chomping/indentation orderings (`|-2` but not `|2-`) — leaving the exact bypass the issue reported reachable via the other ordering. My own hand-written tests all passed (100% coverage, all green) because I only tested the ordering I'd already coded for; I never independently derived the full grammar before writing the regex.
**Root cause:** I anchored the regex on the pattern given in the issue's own "Recommendation" section (`/^[|>][+-]?\d*$/`) without independently verifying it against the actual YAML grammar it's meant to model. Tests written afterward inherited the same blind spot as the regex itself, so they couldn't have caught it — self-authored tests validate that code does what the author intended, not that the author's intent was complete.
**Apply next time:** When a fix is regex/grammar-shaped and security-relevant (a CI gate, a validator, an allow/deny pattern), explicitly enumerate the full grammar/spec — not just the sample an issue happened to give — before finalizing the pattern, and treat an independent review pass (deep-review or otherwise) on exactly this class of change as high-value rather than a formality, since author-written tests share the author's blind spots by construction.

## L-2026-07-18-02 — An issue's own recommended regex fix for a $GITHUB_OUTPUT injection guard was itself line-anchored, not string-anchored, and would not have closed the vulnerability

- **Source**: eva-implement run for ievo-ai/skills#379 (branch `eva-impl/379-github-output-semver-guard`)
- **Signal**: while implementing the issue's literal recommendation — validate `plugin.json`'s attacker-controlled `version` field via `printf '%s' "$new" | grep -qE '^[0-9]+\.[0-9]+\.[0-9]+$'` before writing it to `$GITHUB_OUTPUT` — I manually ran the exact exploit payload from the issue's own exploit chain (`"1.0.0\ninjected=malicious"`) through the guard before trusting it, and it PASSED (was accepted) instead of being rejected. `grep -E '^...$'` anchors `^`/`$` to each LINE of stdin, not to the whole input; a two-line payload whose first line is valid semver satisfies the pattern on that line and `grep -q` returns success. The exact same pattern already existed, unmodified, in this file's pre-existing `workflow_dispatch` branch (which the issue explicitly cited as the guard to "mirror") — so it was independently vulnerable to the same bypass, just gated behind a narrower threat model (an authenticated operator triggering `workflow_dispatch` via the API with a crafted multi-line input).
- **Root cause**: copying an issue's own "Recommendation" section's exact regex/tool invocation without independently re-deriving it against the actual exploit chain in the SAME issue. The issue's author (Eva's own prior vuln-scan run) proposed `grep -qE '^[0-9]+\.[0-9]+\.[0-9]+$'` as the fix, but never verified that pattern against the newline-injection payload that motivated the finding — the recommendation inherited the exact blind spot it was meant to close.
- **Apply next time**: for ANY security-relevant regex/format guard (CI gate, output sanitizer, allow/deny check) — including one pulled verbatim from an issue's own "Recommendation" section — manually run the guard against the literal exploit payload from that issue's "Exploit chain" section before trusting it, not just against the happy-path value. For whole-string validation of a shell variable that may legitimately contain newlines (attacker input, `$(...)` command substitution output), prefer bash's `[[ "$var" =~ ^pattern$ ]]` (anchors to the whole string) over `grep -qE '^pattern$'` piped from `printf`/`echo` (anchors per line) — the two are NOT equivalent despite identical-looking pattern text. This reinforces [[L-2026-07-18-01]] (hand-rolled regex fixes for security gates need independent review) with a sharper, reusable rule: the review must specifically include re-running the ORIGINAL exploit payload, not just a differently-shaped test case.

## L-2026-07-22-01 — Skipping the Phase 1 evolution-store read let an already-captured lesson repeat itself

- **Source**: eva-implement run for ievo-ai/skills#402 (branch `eva-impl/402-vuln-scan-excerpt-containment`)
- **Signal**: my first local gate run (`node --test plugins/ievo/scripts/tests/*.test.mjs`) failed — `evolution_candidates.test.mjs`'s coupling assertion caught that I'd bumped `discover.mjs`'s `SCRIPT_VERSION` and the four AGENTS.md-documented version files, but missed `evolution_candidates.mjs`'s own `SCRIPT_VERSION`. `EVA_EVOLUTION_STORE` already contained `L-2026-07-06-01`, recorded from a prior `ievo-ai/skills#319` run, describing this EXACT gap (AGENTS.md's bump checklist omits `evolution_candidates.mjs`) with the exact same "apply next time" fix (`grep -rn "SCRIPT_VERSION" plugins/ievo/scripts/*.mjs` before trusting the documented file list). I never read the store before starting the build, so the lesson couldn't help.
- **Root cause**: Phase 1 of the eva-implement prompt instructs reading `EVA_EVOLUTION_STORE` before diving into repo research, but the issue's router analysis was detailed and clear enough that I went straight into reading target-repo source/CI files without first checking the store — the store read is easy to skip when the issue itself already feels well-scoped, but the store's value is independent of how clear the issue looks; it protects against repo-specific gotchas the issue text has no reason to mention.
- **Apply next time**: treat the `EVA_EVOLUTION_STORE` read as a hard first step before ANY other repo research, regardless of how clear or narrow the router's analysis already made the issue look — a well-scoped issue and a non-obvious repo gotcha are orthogonal, and only the store catches the latter. This is a process-order fix, not a new fact — the underlying fact is already fully captured in `L-2026-07-06-01`; do not duplicate that entry, just enforce reading it earlier.

## L-2026-07-22-02 — Phase 1's evolution-store read is ordered last-of-five and gets skipped by its own "stop when you have enough" escape hatch

- **Source**: eva-implement run for ievo-ai/skills#364 (branch `eva-impl/364-validator-symlink-safe-read`, cross-repo build)
- **Signal**: `git push -u origin HEAD` landed a `ievo-ai/skills` branch inside `ievo-ai/eva` instead — `origin`'s URL was `.../ievo-ai/eva.git` even though the checked-out working tree content, and everything `git fetch origin` had pulled, was genuinely `ievo-ai/skills`. I fully re-diagnosed this from scratch (compared branch SHAs via `gh api repos/.../branches/main`, inspected `git reflog`, tested the push, caught the wrong destination from git's own "Create a pull request... ievo-ai/eva" remote message, then fixed it with an explicit-URL push and deleted the stray branch via `gh api -X DELETE .../git/refs/heads/...`). Only when writing this capture entry did I finally read `EVA_EVOLUTION_STORE` and find `L-2026-07-15-01` — an entry from a prior `ievo-ai/skills#387` run describing this *exact* scenario, with the *exact* same diagnosis and the *exact* same remediation, word for word. `L-2026-07-22-01` (captured on this same day, for `ievo-ai/skills#402`) had ALREADY flagged this precise meta-failure once — "treat the EVA_EVOLUTION_STORE read as a hard first step" — and it recurred anyway, on the very next implement run.
- **Root cause**: Phase 1 lists the evolution-store read as item 5 of 5 ("stop when you have enough"). Items 1-4 (repo conventions doc, CI workflows, relevant source files, recent merged PRs) are almost always sufficient to feel ready for Phase 2, so item 5 structurally never gets reached — its position in the list, not lack of intent, is what causes the skip. `L-2026-07-22-01`'s fix ("treat it as a hard first step") described the right behavior but didn't change the mechanical habit of reading Phase 1's list in the order given, so the fix didn't actually take on this run.
- **Apply next time**: read `EVA_EVOLUTION_STORE` as the literal FIRST tool call of the entire run — before Phase 0's acknowledgement comment, or immediately after, but strictly before any target-repo file is opened. Do not treat it as list-item-5 of Phase 1's "load context" checklist; treat it as its own zeroth step with no "stop when you have enough" exit. If `L-2026-07-15-01`-style symptoms appear again (a cross-repo `origin` remote resolving to the wrong owner/repo), that itself should be a signal to check the store immediately rather than re-deriving the diagnosis.

## L-2026-07-23-01 — Adding a new escape character to a shared escaping function requires grepping the WHOLE test file for that character, not just the one test the issue names

- **Source**: eva-implement run for ievo-ai/skills#377 (branch `eva-impl/377-escapemdcell-link-image-neutralize`)
- **Signal**: my first local gate run (`node --test plugins/ievo/scripts/tests/*.test.mjs`) failed one test — not the test the issue's own recommendation explicitly named for updating (`tests/scan_repo.test.mjs:129`, the "escapes pipe" unit test, which I did update), but a SEPARATE integration test ("escapes pipe/backtick table-breakout attempts across every plugin table") whose fixture happened to embed a `[pwn](javascript:x)` substring for an unrelated reason (demonstrating pipe-escaping across a full `renderIndexMd()` pass) and whose baked-in exact-string `assert.match` regex still expected the OLD, unescaped `[pwn]` form.
- **Root cause**: I scoped my test-file review to the ONE test location the issue's "Recommendation" section explicitly cited, and trusted that citation as the complete list of tests needing updates. It wasn't — any OTHER test in the same file whose fixture happens to contain the character(s) I was newly escaping (`[`/`!`) would break the same way, regardless of whether that test's *stated purpose* has anything to do with brackets/bangs.
- **Apply next time**: before running the gate, `grep -n '\[' <test-file>` / `grep -n '!' <test-file>` (or whatever characters a new escape step is adding) across the ENTIRE test file being modified — not just the test(s) an issue's recommendation names — and check every hit for a baked-in exact-string assertion that assumes the pre-change escaping behavior. An issue's own recommendation citing "update test X" is a floor, not a ceiling, on which tests need touching.

## L-2026-07-23-02 — A plugin-file PR that omits the mandatory version bump still passes CI: the coupling test catches version DRIFT, not an absent bump

- **Source**: eva-fix-pr run for ievo-ai/skills#414 (branch `eva-impl/372-feedback-title-shell-guard`, cross-repo fix)
- **Signal**: Eva's own REQUEST_CHANGES review on this PR judged the security fix itself correct (`feedback/SKILL.md`'s derived issue title written to a file then passed as a double-quoted `--title "$TITLE"`, neutralizing `$(...)`/backticks/`;`/`&&`) but flagged that the PR shipped WITHOUT the AGENTS.md-mandated in-commit version bump — `marketplace.json` (both version fields), `plugin.json`, `discover.mjs` + `evolution_candidates.mjs` `SCRIPT_VERSION` (0.54.5->0.54.6), the AGENTS.md compliance-ledger header, and a `CHANGELOG.md` entry were all missing. Both product gates (Coverage Gate + Pre-commit Gate) were green, so CI never caught it, and the review noted the original implement run skipped the bump because it was "sandbox-blocked."
- **Root cause**: the `discover.test.mjs` / `evolution_candidates.test.mjs` coupling assertions only check that each script's `SCRIPT_VERSION` EQUALS `plugin.json`'s version — i.e. they detect DRIFT between the version-carrying files, not a version that was simply never bumped. A PR that touches a plugin file (`plugins/ievo/**`, `.claude-plugin/**`) but leaves every version file untouched keeps them all mutually consistent at the old value, so the coupling test passes and the omission is invisible to the merge gate. AGENTS.md documents this exact limitation ("The coupling assertion in `discover.test.mjs` catches drift on the coverage-gate"), but the implementer treated the security fix as the whole task and, blocked or not, never completed the mandatory same-commit bump.
- **Apply next time**: whenever an eva-implement/eva-fix PR modifies ANY file under `plugins/ievo/**` or `.claude-plugin/**` (a plugin-file PR, not an infra/docs-only one — those deliberately do NOT bump), treat the AGENTS.md version bump as a non-optional, same-commit part of the change: bump `marketplace.json` (metadata.version + plugins[0].version), `plugin.json`, and EVERY `plugins/ievo/scripts/*.mjs` `SCRIPT_VERSION` (grep for all of them — see [[L-2026-07-06-01]], the checklist is not exhaustive), update the AGENTS.md compliance-ledger header, and add a reverse-chronological `CHANGELOG.md` entry. Never treat "both gates are green" as evidence the bump was done — the coupling test only flags drift, not an absent bump. And if a sandbox/tooling limitation blocks part of a change, do not silently ship the partial PR: complete it or surface the blocker, because an incomplete-but-internally-consistent PR sails through every gate.

## L-2026-07-23-03 — Third recurrence of the evolution_candidates.mjs SCRIPT_VERSION coupling-test failure, despite two prior meta-fixes telling future runs to read the store first

- **Source**: eva-implement run for ievo-ai/skills#378 (branch `eva-impl/378-frontmatter-control-char-strip`)
- **Signal**: my first local gate run (`node --test plugins/ievo/scripts/tests/*.test.mjs`) failed — `evolution_candidates.test.mjs`'s coupling assertion caught that I'd bumped `discover.mjs`'s `SCRIPT_VERSION` plus the four AGENTS.md-documented version files, but missed `evolution_candidates.mjs`'s own `SCRIPT_VERSION`. This is the exact same gap `[[L-2026-07-06-01]]` recorded from `ievo-ai/skills#319`, that `[[L-2026-07-22-01]]` recorded recurring on `ievo-ai/skills#402`, and that a `[[L-2026-07-23-02]]` review finding independently reconfirmed on `ievo-ai/skills#414`. I had not read `EVA_EVOLUTION_STORE` before starting this build either — same root cause `[[L-2026-07-22-02]]` already diagnosed in exhaustive detail, with an explicit "read it as the literal first tool call, before Phase 0's ack" remediation.
- **Root cause**: two prior entries (`L-2026-07-22-01`, `L-2026-07-22-02`) already prescribed reading `EVA_EVOLUTION_STORE` before any other action, specifically to prevent this exact class of repeat. Neither prevented this run's repeat. A written "apply next time" instruction living inside the very file that gets skipped is a fragile fix — it only works if the agent reads the store, but the failure mode IS not reading the store, so the fix and the failure share the same blind spot by construction. Instruction-based remediation recorded in the store cannot bootstrap its own compliance.
- **Apply next time**: for this specific fact (bump ALL `SCRIPT_VERSION` occurrences, not just `discover.mjs`), the durable fix is NOT another store entry — it is moving the check out of prose and into something mechanically enforced regardless of whether any agent reads memory first. Concretely: `.github/scripts/check-coverage.mjs` or a small dedicated pre-commit/CI validator should assert that every `export const SCRIPT_VERSION` literal under `plugins/ievo/scripts/*.mjs` is identical to `plugin.json`'s version whenever `plugins/ievo/**` is touched — the way `discover.test.mjs`'s own coupling test already does for that one file. Recommend the operator (or a future issue) file this as a real repo change in `ievo-ai/skills`, not rely on a further evolution-store entry, since the store-based remediation path has now been tried twice and failed twice.

## L-2026-07-23-04 — Dogfooding `/ievo:evo` for Eva's OWN Phase-4d lesson capture is unsafe specifically when $TARGET_REPO is ievo-ai/skills itself

- **Source**: eva-implement run for ievo-ai/skills#378 (branch `eva-impl/378-frontmatter-control-char-strip`)
- **Signal**: the eva-implement prompt's Phase 4d says "if `EVA_IEVO_PLUGIN_READY` is true, invoke the `/ievo:evolution` skill to record [the lesson]" instead of hand-appending to `EVA_EVOLUTION_CAPTURE`. Reading `plugins/ievo/skills/evo/SKILL.md` (this repo IS the skill's own source) before invoking it, project-wide-scope capture (Step 3) injects a `<!-- ievo:start -->` marker block into `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md` if none exists yet — it doesn't, in this repo — and creates a new `.ievo/evolution/project.md`. Both are writes to files the issue (a `parseFrontmatter` CWE-150 fix, unrelated to AGENTS.md or `.ievo/`) never asked for.
- **Root cause**: the master prompt's dogfooding instructions assume the iEvo plugin is a third-party tool installed for Eva's convenience on an arbitrary target repo — true for every other repo Eva builds in, but `ievo-ai/skills` IS the plugin's own source repo, so "dogfood the evolution-capture skill" collides with "never modify files outside the issue's scope": the skill's own normal, correct behavior (marker injection + overlay file) is exactly the kind of scope creep the Safety rules forbid when the repo being built in is the skill's own home.
- **Apply next time**: on any eva-implement/eva-fix-pr run where `$TARGET_REPO == ievo-ai/skills`, skip invoking `/ievo:evo`/`ievo:evolution` for Eva's OWN Phase 4d/Phase 6 lesson capture — append directly to `EVA_EVOLUTION_CAPTURE` instead (the mechanism Phase 6's consolidation PR actually reads from) and note in the PR body that the skill invocation was skipped for this reason. This is unrelated to Phase 4.5's `/ievo:deep-review`/`/ievo:security-check` calls (those only READ the diff, no file-write side effect) — this caveat is specific to `/ievo:evo`'s write-oriented capture flow.


## L-2026-07-24-01 — A first-party App-token permission-* mint can request a permission the App installation doesn't actually have, and no local gate catches it

- **Source**: eva-implement run for ievo-ai/eva#231 (branch `eva-impl/231-reaper-stale-claims`)
- **Signal**: `/ievo:deep-review` on my own diff (before opening the PR) flagged that the new `eva-reaper.yml`'s `Generate App token` step requested `permission-actions: read` — but `eva-ci-failure.yml`, an existing sibling workflow in this same repo, explicitly documents (in its own header comment) that the `ievo-eva` App installation's `actions` grant is unconfirmed, and per eva#152 a mint requesting an ungranted permission fails LOUDLY at the mint step. Since that mint step runs unconditionally on every scheduled tick (not gated behind the workflow's own `EVA_REAPER_ENABLED` dormant-by-default flag), this would have broken every hourly run immediately after merge — contradicting the workflow's own "merges inert" safety claim. Neither `uv run ruff check`, `uv run pytest --cov`, nor `actionlint` (this repo's actual local quality gate) could ever catch this — it's a GitHub App installation-configuration fact, invisible to any static check or test running outside GitHub's own Actions runtime.
- **Root cause**: I designed the new workflow's App-token permission list by reasoning purely from "what does this script's logic need" (`gh run list` needs `actions:read`) without first checking whether a SIBLING workflow in the same repo had already discovered and documented a constraint on what the App installation is actually granted. `docs/GITHUB_APP_SETUP.md` (the nominal source of truth) is stale and predates several permissions already in live production use, so it gave no signal either way.
- **Apply next time**: before adding `permission-<X>: <access>` to any NEW `actions/create-github-app-token` mint, `grep -rn "permission-<X>\|<X> permission\|<X>.*grant" .github/workflows/*.yml` across the repo's EXISTING workflows first — a sibling workflow that already needed the same permission and hit a wall (documented in its own header comments, e.g. `eva-ci-failure.yml`'s "the App installation's `actions` permission is NOT confirmed granted") is a stronger, already-empirically-tested signal than the setup doc or first-principles reasoning about what the script needs. If a sibling workflow deliberately routes a specific call through the PAT instead of the App token, mirror that exact split rather than assuming the App can just be asked for the missing grant.

## L-2026-07-24-17 — Relocating a security-relevant script from outside the repo to a project-local copy must establish its tamper-containment (gitignore) BEFORE the copy, not as a later step

- **Source**: eva-fix-pr run for ievo-ai/skills#430 (branch `eva-impl/424-evo-auto-failure-capture`, cross-repo fix)
- **Signal**: Eva's own REQUEST_CHANGES review on this PR (the implementer had drafted both fixes but lost Edit/Write tool access mid skill-pass and shipped them noted-but-unapplied) flagged two in-scope gaps. (1) `evo-auto-enable`'s Step 3.5.1 vendored `scrub.mjs` — the privacy-redaction engine itself — into a project-local `.ievo/hooks/scripts/vendor/` copy, while the only `.ievo/hooks/` gitignore handling sat two sub-steps later in Step 3.5.4 as a soft "offer to add it". So between the copy and that later offer, the redaction engine lived at a git-trackable path where a PR could commit and then alter it to defeat scrubbing — a real escalation versus the old baked-`CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT` cache fallback, which pointed *outside* the git tree and so was never committable/PR-tamperable. (2) The PR's new `PostToolUseFailure`/`PermissionDenied` failure-capture hook was not added to `security-check`'s first-party carve-out (SKILL.md:256 named only `UserPromptSubmit`), so iEvo's own security auditor would self-flag iEvo's own hook.
- **Root cause**: the PR replaced a fallback that lived in the plugin cache *outside* the git tree with a project-local vendored copy *inside* it — a relocation that silently crossed a trust boundary (the redaction engine went from never-committable to committable), but the containment guarantee that neutralizes the new exposure (gitignoring `.ievo/hooks/`) was authored as a downstream convenience step rather than a precondition of the copy. The window between the copy and a deferred guard IS the exposure. Separately, adding a new hook event type to a first-party skill wasn't mirrored into the tool that audits hooks, so the auditor's known-exception list lagged the product it audits.
- **Apply next time**: (a) whenever a fallback/vendoring change moves a security-relevant artifact (a redaction engine, a secret, a validator) from outside the repo to a git-trackable in-repo path, front-load the tamper-containment guarantee (gitignore / checksum / read-only) as a HARD precondition immediately before the write — never as a later "offer to add it" step; treat the relocation itself as a trust-boundary change, not a mechanical path swap. (b) when a skill adds a NEW hook event type, grep `security-check`'s carve-out (and its hook-abuse pattern list) for the existing first-party exceptions and extend them to the new hook in the SAME PR, so iEvo's own auditor doesn't self-flag iEvo's own hook.

## L-2026-07-24-18 — ievo-ai/skills version-bump checklist is stale relative to enforced coupling tests

- **Source:** ievo-ai/skills#429 (hooks-setup platform-compat sweep) — implementer build.
- **Signal:** After bumping the 4 files AGENTS.md § "Version bumping — in every PR" documents (`marketplace.json`, `plugin.json`, `discover.mjs` `SCRIPT_VERSION`, AGENTS.md compliance-ledger header) and re-running `node plugins/ievo/scripts/validate_skills.mjs` (green), the full `node --test plugins/ievo/scripts/tests/*.test.mjs` gate failed 2 tests: `evolution_candidates.test.mjs` and `scrub.test.mjs` each assert their own script's `SCRIPT_VERSION` equals `plugin.json`'s version — neither script is in AGENTS.md's documented 4-file list.
- **Root cause:** `evolution_candidates.mjs` (v0.45.0) and `scrub.mjs` (v0.55.0) each shipped with their own `SCRIPT_VERSION` constant and a dedicated coupling test mirroring `discover.test.mjs`'s original pattern, but AGENTS.md's canonical version-bump checklist was never updated to list them — doc/enforcement drift, not a one-off mistake. (`scan_repo.mjs` is correctly NOT in scope — its `SCRIPT_VERSION` is an intentionally decoupled scanner-output-format semver, per v0.6.6/#47.)
- **Apply next time:** Before a version-bump PR in `ievo-ai/skills`, run `grep -rn "export const SCRIPT_VERSION" plugins/ievo/scripts/*.mjs` and bump every match except `scan_repo.mjs` to the new version, then run the FULL `node --test plugins/ievo/scripts/tests/*.test.mjs` suite (not just `validate_skills.mjs`) before treating the gate as green. Don't trust AGENTS.md's documented file list as exhaustive without cross-checking the actual coupling tests it's meant to describe.

## L-2026-07-24-02 — A `paths:`-style activation glob is root-anchored unless `**/`-prefixed, and "it matches in THIS repo" is not evidence it matches in a user's

- **Source**: eva-fix-pr run for ievo-ai/skills#443 (branch `eva-impl/157-agent-effort-frontmatter-sweep`)
- **Signal**: eva-review-pr flagged as a blocker that `security-check/SKILL.md`'s new `paths:` list contained bare `plugins/**` and `agents/**`. Claude Code's skill docs define `paths` as "the same format as [memory] path-specific rules", and that page's own pattern table reads `*.md` as "Markdown files in the **project root**". So both patterns were root-anchored and matched nothing in any project that does not vendor `plugins/`/`agents/` at its repo root — i.e. every consumer project, since iEvo installs to `.claude/skills/`. The patterns looked correct only because `ievo-ai/skills` itself happens to have `plugins/` at its root, which is exactly the environment they were written and eyeballed in.
- **Root cause**: I cited the docs page for the FIELD's existence and forward-compat behaviour but never followed the one-hop link to the page that defines its MATCH SEMANTICS, and then sanity-checked the globs against the repo I was standing in rather than against the deployment target. Sibling patterns in the same list (`**/SKILL.md`, `**/AGENTS.md`) were already `**/`-prefixed, so the file contained its own counter-example and the inconsistency still read as fine.
- **Apply next time**: when adding any path/glob-matching config field, resolve the exact matching semantics from the spec that defines them — including one hop through a "same format as X" reference — before writing a single pattern, and state the anchoring rule in the doc line you add. Then test each pattern against a *consumer* layout, not the authoring repo: for a plugin/skill this repo ships, ask "where does this file live once installed into someone else's project?" A directory that can appear at any depth needs `**/`; a genuinely root-fixed one (`.claude/settings.json`) does not. An unprefixed pattern sitting next to `**/`-prefixed siblings in the same list is a live smell, not a style nit.

## L-2026-07-24-03 — Do not scope a deliberately domain-agnostic tool behind an allowlist; a silent non-activation is worse than no gate

- **Source**: eva-fix-pr run for ievo-ai/skills#443 (branch `eva-impl/157-agent-effort-frontmatter-sweep`)
- **Signal**: eva-review-pr's second blocker: the new `paths:` on `vuln-scan/SKILL.md` gated a language-agnostic CWE scanner behind `src/**`, `lib/**`, `**/*.ts`, `**/*.js`, `**/*.mjs`, `**/*.py`, `**/*.go` — killing auto-activation for Rust, Java, Ruby, PHP, C# and every other unlisted ecosystem, and missing `.tsx` even inside the one it did list. The review offered "widen or drop"; widening only moves the boundary, since no finite extension list can cover a taxonomy defined to be language-independent, so the field was dropped.
- **Root cause**: I applied the new field mechanically to the skills that "obviously" had a file-context signal, without testing each candidate against the criterion I had just written into AGENTS.md myself in the same commit — "only where file-context is genuinely predictive; wrong gating is worse than none." A tool whose own description claims universality is by construction always potentially relevant, so it belongs in the same excluded bucket as `evo`/`handoff`/`feedback`, and I had already built that bucket without checking `vuln-scan` against it.
- **Apply next time**: before adding an activation/scoping filter to a component, read that component's own stated scope first. If it advertises itself as language-, framework-, or domain-agnostic, the filter is wrong by construction — drop it rather than enumerate. Run every candidate through the exclusion criterion written in the same change, including the ones that felt obvious. And weigh the failure asymmetry: for a security or safety tool, a false negative (never offers itself) is silent and costly, while a false positive (offers itself in an irrelevant session) is visible and cheap — so bias against gating.

## L-2026-07-24-04 — Before gating a skill on file context, enumerate its PROGRAMMATIC consumers: a preloaded / agent-loaded skill is reached before any target file is in context

- **Source**: eva-fix-pr run for ievo-ai/skills#443 (branch `eva-impl/157-agent-effort-frontmatter-sweep`, round 3 — third consecutive blocker on the same new `paths:` field)
- **Signal**: eva-review-pr's third blocker: `paths:` on `security-check/SKILL.md` (`**/plugins/**`, `**/SKILL.md`, `**/marketplace.json`, …) reads as a textbook "file-context is genuinely predictive" case — reviewing plugin/skill files IS the install-review context. But `security-check` has two consumers that reach it *before* any candidate file exists in context: `evolution.md` preloads it via `skills:` sub-agent frontmatter for its Step 2.5 vendor-time re-audit (#357), and `security-auditor.md` Step 1 loads it through the host platform's skills system at the top of a fresh sub-agent — the candidate clone/read is its Step 2. Claude Code's docs define `paths` as limiting when Claude "loads the skill automatically" and say preloading "draws from the same set of skills Claude can invoke", but neither page states whether the file-context filter is applied on the preload / sub-agent-invocation paths. Unverifiable either way → the gate could silently strip the #357 antivirus audit, so the field was dropped (same failure-asymmetry call as L-2026-07-24-03's `vuln-scan`).
- **Root cause**: I evaluated the new field against ONE surface only — description-match auto-activation in a human's session — and asked "is file context predictive here?" without enumerating the skill's non-auto-activation entry points, where the gate's precondition is unsatisfiable by construction. The evidence was already in the repo and in the very file I was documenting: `AGENTS.md` § Skills format's `disable-model-invocation` bullet, one line above the `paths` bullet I wrote, already records this exact constraint for this exact skill ("Do NOT set it on skills that sub-agents load via the Skill tool / skills system (`security-check` ← `security-auditor`)"), and extends it to `vuln-scan` for the `skills:`-preload variant. I wrote the new field's guidance directly beneath a bullet that spelled out the programmatic-consumer hazard for the same file and did not carry it across.
- **Apply next time**: before adding ANY activation-scoping/gating field to a skill (`paths`, `disable-model-invocation`, a future equivalent), first enumerate every programmatic consumer — `grep -rn "<skill-name>" plugins/*/agents/ plugins/*/skills/ plugins/*/commands/` for `skills:` frontmatter preloads, "use/apply the `<skill>` skill" agent steps, and dispatch chains — then, for each, ask whether the gate's precondition is satisfiable AT THE MOMENT that consumer reaches the skill (a fresh sub-agent's Step 1 has an empty file context by definition). If the platform docs don't explicitly state that the filter is bypassed on the preload / programmatic path, treat it as unverified and don't gate — for a security-critical skill an unverifiable gate is indistinguishable from a silent removal. And read the ADJACENT doc bullets about the same file before adding a sibling field: a constraint already recorded for one gating field almost always transfers to the next one, and it is stronger evidence than reasoning from the new field's docs alone.

## L-2026-07-24-05 — When a review rejects one instance of a newly-introduced field, re-audit EVERY remaining instance in the same round, not just the one named

- **Source**: eva-fix-pr run for ievo-ai/skills#443 (branch `eva-impl/157-agent-effort-frontmatter-sweep`, round 4 — FOURTH consecutive blocker on the same new `paths:` field)
- **Signal**: eva-review-pr rejected `paths:` on `vuln-scan` (round 1), then `security-check` (round 3), then `hooks-setup` AND `index-repos` (round 4). Every round I fixed exactly the skill(s) the review named, re-derived the criterion in AGENTS.md, and left the field's remaining call sites unexamined — so the same class of defect surfaced again the next round, burning 4 of a 5-round budget on one field that ultimately belongs on ZERO skills. Round 4's two rejections were falsified by evidence sitting in the very files I was editing, below the frontmatter: `hooks-setup`'s own Step 4 treats an absent `.claude/settings.json` as `{}` and Step 8 has a Write branch to create it (so the gated file is missing precisely in the first-run session the skill exists for), and `index-repos`' own `## Input` says its subject is a REMOTE `<owner>/<repo>` shallow-cloned into `~/.ievo/checkouts/` (so no local file is evidence of relevance).
- **Root cause**: I treated each review finding as a point defect about one named component instead of as evidence that my *criterion* for the new field was wrong. Each round I sharpened the rule in the doc and then failed to re-run the sharpened rule against the other components already carrying the field in the same commit — the cheapest possible check, since the diff lists them. Compounding it, I validated each remaining gate by asking "is file context plausibly predictive here?" (a confirming question, always answerable yes) rather than "is this gate's precondition satisfiable at the moment a user actually wants this skill?" (a falsifying one), and never read past the frontmatter into the body of the file I was editing, where both counter-examples were written down.
- **Apply next time**: when a review rejects one use of a field/pattern the SAME change introduced, treat it as a criterion failure, not an instance failure: immediately `grep` the diff for every other use of that field and re-run each against the sharpened criterion in the same fix round, then state in the PR comment that you did. Ask the falsifying question — "when does this precondition NOT hold, and is that the case the component is FOR?" — and read the component's own body (its `## Input`, its first step, its file-absent branch) before trusting frontmatter you are adding to it: a skill's body routinely contains the counter-example to its own gate. If re-auditing leaves the field on nothing, that is a valid, honest outcome — ship it as an evaluated negative with the criteria documented rather than keeping one token instance to make the change look non-empty.

## L-2026-07-24-06 — A "sweep" is defined by a component set enumerated from the filesystem, not by the list in the proposal issue; and the convention doc must record the CRITERION, not the resulting list

- **Source**: eva-fix-pr run for ievo-ai/skills#443 (branch `eva-impl/157-agent-effort-frontmatter-sweep`)
- **Signal**: eva-review-pr flagged the `argument-hint` set as incomplete: `security-check` documents a `<owner>/<repo>@<skill>` identifier under its own `## Input` and got no hint, and `plugins/ievo/commands/` was untouched entirely even though `commands/vuln-scan.md` documents a `--pr <number>` / `--module <path>` scope-mode table. Claude Code's docs state custom commands were merged into skills — `commands/deploy.md` and `skills/deploy/SKILL.md` "both create `/deploy` and work the same way" and read the same frontmatter table — so `commands/` was never a different surface, just a directory I did not `ls`. The review also flagged that AGENTS.md recorded the outcome as "Current set: `inspect`, `feedback`, `index-repos`, `handoff`", freezing the gap as a documented convention and making the omission read as deliberate to the next contributor.
- **Root cause**: I scoped the sweep to the four components the proposal issue (#177) happened to name, and took "skills" literally as `plugins/ievo/skills/*/SKILL.md`, never enumerating the sibling `commands/` and `agents/` directories to ask which of them the field also applies to. Then I wrote the *result* of my incomplete pass into the conventions doc as the convention itself — a hardcoded name list, which cannot be checked against reality and silently ratifies whatever was missed.
- **Apply next time**: for any change described as a "sweep"/"across all X", first enumerate X from the filesystem (`ls plugins/*/skills plugins/*/commands plugins/*/agents`), then apply one written criterion to every entry and record the per-entry verdict — including the deliberate NOs and why (e.g. `skills/vuln-scan/SKILL.md` takes its `## Input` from an agent dispatch, not a user, so its hint belongs on the `commands/` entry point instead). Verify whether two directories are actually distinct surfaces before treating one as out of scope — check the platform docs, which may have merged them. And in the convention doc, state the RULE first and derive the list from it ("every component whose body documents a user-supplied argument carries one"), so a future gap is a rule violation a reader can detect rather than an entry silently missing from a frozen list.

## L-2026-07-24-07 — Issue-proposed wording can embed stale facts about the repo itself; verify against source before copying into new prose

- **Source**: eva-implement run for ievo-ai/skills#203 (Cursor compat sweep)
- **Signal**: My own CHANGELOG.md entry stated `/ievo:deep-review` has a "structured 10-point checklist." The repo's own SKILL.md description, compatibility-field precedent, and `deep-reviewer.md` agent all consistently say "11-point" (a Point 11 — leaked secrets — was added in a later release than the checklist's original count). Caught only by the `/ievo:deep-review` pass before commit, not by any earlier step.
- **Root cause**: the "10-point" phrasing came from the issue's own `## Proposed solution` sentence, which I trusted as accurate since the issue author had already carefully verified the *external* Cursor claims cited elsewhere in the issue. I wrote the actual `deep-review/SKILL.md` compatibility field without a number (avoiding the drift there), but reused the issue's number from memory when drafting the unrelated CHANGELOG prose, instead of grepping the current skill body.
- **Apply next time**: when an issue's proposed wording includes a specific fact about the REPO ITSELF (a count, a version, a field name) — as opposed to an external claim the issue already verified — grep the current source for that fact before writing it into ANY new file, even a PR description or CHANGELOG entry that isn't literally copying the issue's suggested text. External-fact verification (Phase 2 WebFetch/WebSearch) and internal-fact verification are separate checks; doing one does not cover the other.

## L-2026-07-24-08 — `node --test <dir>` silently fails to discover tests; a repo's CI workflow YAML is the only trustworthy source for the exact local dry-run command

- **Source**: eva-implement run for ievo-ai/skills#203 (Cursor compat sweep), Phase 4d quality gate
- **Signal**: `node --test --experimental-test-coverage plugins/ievo/scripts/tests/` (bare directory arg, matching AGENTS.md's prose shorthand for the command) exits with `Error: Cannot find module '.../tests'` / `MODULE_NOT_FOUND` — it does not discover `*.test.mjs` files inside the directory on Node v22.23.1. `.github/workflows/coverage-gate.yml` instead passes an explicit glob (`plugins/ievo/scripts/tests/*.test.mjs`) plus `--test-reporter=lcov`/`--test-reporter=spec` flags, which runs correctly (640/640 tests pass, 100/100/100 on every gated script via `.github/scripts/check-coverage.mjs`).
- **Root cause**: Node's built-in test runner needs a glob or explicit file list to pick up `*.test.mjs` files from a directory arg — AGENTS.md's documented shorthand doesn't match what CI actually runs, and the failure mode (module-not-found) looks nothing like a real coverage/test failure, so it's easy to misdiagnose as "the repo is broken" rather than "my invocation is wrong."
- **Apply next time**: for this repo (and generally, before trusting a CLAUDE.md/AGENTS.md-documented test command), read the actual `.github/workflows/*.yml` invocation and reproduce it verbatim for a local dry-run — the workflow YAML is the enforced contract; prose shorthand in a docs file can silently drift from it. Confirm the gate the same way CI does (`check-coverage.mjs coverage.lcov`), not just "tests passed."

## L-2026-07-24-09 — A per-agent budget field must be set by the agent's most demanding BRANCH, not its dominant one — and the file's own step list is the source of truth, not the PR's intent

- **Source**: eva-fix-pr run for ievo-ai/skills#443 (branch `eva-impl/157-agent-effort-frontmatter-sweep`, cross-repo fix)
- **Signal**: Eva's own REQUEST_CHANGES review flagged that `plugins/ievo/agents/evolution.md` was pinned `effort: low` while its **Step 2.5** applies `security-check`'s full threat-pattern deep-scan + GREEN/YELLOW/RED verdict to freshly-vendored content before that content lands in `.claude/agents/`/`.claude/skills/` — the exact antivirus guarantee the SAME PR pinned `high` on `security-auditor.md`/`vuln-scanner.md` to protect. `effort` is per-agent, not per-step, so `low` actively downgraded that audit even for a high-effort caller session. The tell was visible inside the frontmatter I was editing: the very next key, `skills: - ievo:security-check`, exists solely to preload the antivirus methodology "for Step 2.5's re-audit gate", and my own rationale comment narrowed itself to "Steps 2-4" — naming a subset while an un-named Step 2.5 sat between 2 and 3. A second, same-shaped finding in the same review: the CHANGELOG summarized `hooks-setup`'s new `paths:` as `` (`.claude/settings*`) `` while the file actually lists `.claude/settings.json`, `.claude/settings.local.json` AND `**/SKILL.md`.
- **Root cause**: both findings are the same mistake at two altitudes — I characterized an artifact from the plan's one-line intent ("evolution = mechanical overlay append"; "hooks-setup gates settings files") instead of reading back what the artifact actually contains. For the effort pin I classified the agent by its *dominant/nominal* job rather than enumerating every branch it can execute and taking the max; a rarely-reached conditional security gate is invisible to a description-level summary but is precisely the branch a reasoning-budget floor exists to protect. For the CHANGELOG I transcribed the intent I had when adding the field rather than the final value list on disk.
- **Apply next time**: when assigning ANY per-agent capability/budget field (`effort`, `model`, tool grants) in an agent file, grep that file's own `^## Step` / `^## ` headings and set the field by the MOST demanding branch, not the most frequent one — a conditional security/audit gate anywhere in the body sets the floor for the whole agent, since these fields have no per-step granularity. Treat a rationale comment that names a step *range* ("Steps 2-4", "the main path") as a self-smell: it is an admission that other steps exist and were not considered — read them before shipping. And when a CHANGELOG/PR-body bullet summarizes a frontmatter list you added, re-read the final block on disk and enumerate its actual values rather than restating the intent, since the list often grew after the sentence was written. Cross-check against the sibling files the same PR touches ([[L-2026-07-24-01]]'s "a sibling that already solved this is the stronger signal" rule applies here too: `security-auditor.md`'s and `vuln-scanner.md`'s "pinned high so a low-effort caller can't silently degrade the guarantee" comments were the ready-made test my `evolution.md` pin failed).

## L-2026-07-24-10 — A PR body's "found and fixed" claim was trusted instead of re-verified, and the Summary went stale across four fix rounds

- **Source**: eva-fix-pr run for ievo-ai/skills#443 (branch `eva-impl/157-agent-effort-frontmatter-sweep`, cross-repo fix, round 5/5)
- **Signal**: Eva's own REQUEST_CHANGES review found `CHANGELOG.md`'s v0.61.0 summary line still 403 chars / two sentences, violating AGENTS.md § Changelog's "one sentence, hard-capped at ~240 characters" rule (which `cut-release.yml` copies verbatim into the public Release body, #306) — while the PR body's `## iEvo skill pass` section explicitly stated that `/ievo:deep-review` had found this exact issue and that it was "fixed on this branch". The review's own words: "The PR body claims this was already fixed — it isn't." The review separately flagged that the body's `## Summary` still described `paths:` as applied to `index-repos`/`hooks-setup` — rounds `[pr-fix-1]`…`[pr-fix-3]` had narrowed that set to **none**. A third bullet claiming `argument-hint:` on "the 4 argument-taking skills" was equally stale (the branch carries 9), though the review only named the `paths:` one.
- **Root cause**: two coupled failures. (1) A self-authored note recording "deep-review found X, fixed on this branch" was written into the PR body as the record of the fix, but the corresponding file edit either never landed or was lost in a later round — and no round re-verified the claim against the actual file. A prose claim about a fix is not evidence of the fix. (2) The PR body's `## Summary` is authored once at implement time and describes the *first pass*; every subsequent `[pr-fix-N]` round mutated the branch's substance (the `paths:` set shrank to empty, the `argument-hint:` set grew from 4 to 9) but only ever touched code — nothing in the fix loop re-derives the Summary from the branch's current state, so the body drifts further from reality with every round that "successfully" fixes something.
- **Apply next time**: (a) never treat a PR-body/CHANGELOG sentence asserting "issue X was found and fixed" as evidence — re-verify it mechanically against the file before letting it stand (here: `python3 -c "print(len(open('CHANGELOG.md').read().split(chr(10))[10]))"` against the documented ~240 cap), and if the claim is false, either land the fix or delete the claim. (b) On EVERY `eva-fix-pr` round, diff the PR body's `## Summary` against the branch's actual state for each factual claim it makes (`grep -rn "^paths:" plugins/ievo/`, `grep -rn "^argument-hint:" plugins/ievo/`) and `gh pr edit --body-file` it back into agreement — the body is a public artefact the reviewer reads as a claim about the diff, so a stale Summary is itself a review finding, and fixing only the code half of a "body is stale" finding guarantees another round. Correcting adjacent stale claims in the SAME Summary section is completing the named finding, not scope creep.

## L-2026-07-24-11 — eva-fix-pr's "do NOT change git remotes" safety rule directly contradicts the mandatory cross-repo origin-remote fix

- **Source**: eva-fix-pr run for ievo-ai/skills#443 (cross-repo fix)
- **Signal**: the wrong-`origin` hazard recorded in `[[L-2026-07-15-01]]` and `[[L-2026-07-22-02]]` recurred exactly as described — `git remote get-url origin` resolved to `https://.../ievo-ai/eva.git` while the working tree was genuinely `ievo-ai/skills`' PR head (local `HEAD` == the PR's `head.sha`), so the prompt's own literal `git push origin HEAD` would have landed the branch in the wrong repo. `git ls-remote origin` proved it (returned only eva's `main`, no `eva-impl/157-*` ref). It also silently corrupted the Phase 2 budget count: the prompt's own `git fetch origin "$BASE"` repointed `refs/remotes/origin/main` at **eva's** unrelated `main`, so `git log origin/main..HEAD | grep -c '\[pr-fix-N\]'` returned **43** instead of 4 — which, read literally against `FIX_BUDGET=5`, would have aborted a legitimate round-5 fix as "budget exhausted".
- **Root cause**: unlike the eva-implement prompt, the eva-fix-pr prompt carries an explicit safety rule — "Do NOT change tokens or git remotes" — in its auth/identity preamble. Read literally, that rule forbids `git remote set-url origin`, which is the exact remediation the evolution store prescribes for this environment bug. The rule's real intent is "don't swap the auth principal / don't retarget the push somewhere unintended", and repointing `origin` at `$TARGET_REPO` with the SAME `$GH_TOKEN` serves that intent rather than violating it — but an agent reading the rule literally would leave the broken remote in place and push to the wrong repo.
- **Apply next time**: on any cross-repo `eva-fix-pr` run (`$TARGET_REPO != ievo-ai/eva`), verify `git remote get-url origin` against `$TARGET_REPO` **before** Phase 2's budget count, not just before the Phase 6 push — the budget arithmetic depends on `origin/main` being the right repo's `main`, so a wrong remote can fake budget exhaustion and produce a spurious operator hand-off. Fix it with `git remote set-url origin "https://x-access-token:${GH_TOKEN}@github.com/$TARGET_REPO.git"` and treat that as compliant with "do NOT change git remotes" (same token, remote corrected to the repo the prompt already names as the target), then state the correction explicitly in the round's PR comment so the operator can see it. Cross-check the budget count against `gh pr view --json commits` if `origin/main..HEAD` returns an implausibly large number. Recommend the operator amend the eva-fix-pr prompt to carve this out explicitly, since a store entry cannot override a rule the prompt states as non-negotiable.

## L-2026-07-24-12 — In a platform-compat doc sweep, the `compatibility:` frontmatter field is the primary surface; a body-prose note alone leaves it contradicting itself

- **Source**: eva-fix-pr run for ievo-ai/skills#444 (branch `eva-impl/203-cursor-compat-sweep`, cross-repo fix)
- **Signal**: Eva's own REQUEST_CHANGES review flagged that `schedule/SKILL.md`'s `compatibility:` field still read "Claude Code only … Codex and other platforms: use the CI cron fallback" even though the same commit had added a Cursor v3.8 `/automate` paragraph to that skill's body. On the sibling skill in the very same sweep (`deep-review/SKILL.md`), the implementer HAD correctly updated `compatibility:`. Net effect: a Cursor v3.8 user reading the authoritative machine-readable field is still routed to the CI-cron fallback, directly contradicting the body prose two screens below it.
- **Root cause**: the implementer followed each absorbed issue's own framing per-file instead of deriving one rule for the sweep. #203 (deep-review) literally proposed a `compatibility:`-field edit, so that file got one; #220 (schedule) was scoped down by the router to "a single sentence in the existing fallback section", so that file got a body paragraph only. Per-issue framing is about where the *narrative* goes; it is not a statement about which surfaces carry the platform-routing contract. `compatibility:` is the agentskills.io-spec field platform routing actually reads, so a body-only note is strictly weaker and can openly contradict it.
- **Apply next time**: when a build's theme is platform compatibility (a "compat sweep", a new platform's native command, a version-gated capability), enumerate EVERY touched skill's `compatibility:` field as a required edit surface up front — before writing any body prose — and diff the field's claims against the new body text for contradiction. Treat a per-issue scope-down ("just one sentence in the fallback section") as guidance on prose volume, not as permission to skip the frontmatter field. Budget for the 500-char `COMPATIBILITY_MAX_LENGTH`: an existing field near the cap must be *rewritten* (trim now-redundant clauses, scope over-broad openers like "Claude Code only" to the specific sub-feature they actually constrain), not merely appended to.

## L-2026-07-24-13 — Cursor's generic `cursor.com/changelog` listing page paginates dated entries out; verify a Cursor claim against its dated permalink

- **Source**: eva-fix-pr run for ievo-ai/skills#444 (branch `eva-impl/203-cursor-compat-sweep`, cross-repo fix)
- **Signal**: the review's third finding asked for in-build confirmation of the `/automate` and `.cursor/environment.json` claims, which the implement run had shipped on the operator's verification trail alone — its own changelog check "didn't reach those specific dated entries". Reproduced exactly: a WebFetch of `https://cursor.com/changelog` returned **0 of 4** target features (only Jul 22 → Jun 29 2026 entries were on page 1; the nav's `/automate` and `/bugbot` links are product pages, not changelog entries). This is the same wall Eva's own earlier note on #203 described — "my first pass flagged this as fabricated because the generic listing page only shows a June 17 '3.7' entry unrelated to `/review`". A WebSearch immediately surfaced the dated permalinks, and all three claims then verified verbatim against primary sources: `/automate` + "Workflow run completed" GitHub trigger at `cursor.com/changelog/06-18-26` (3.8, Jun 18 2026); reusable env snapshot + "benefits your entire team when committed to `.cursor/environment.json`" + `/in-cloud` at `cursor.com/changelog/cloud-in-agents-window` (3.7, Jun 17 2026); `/review` at `cursor.com/changelog/bugbot-updates-june-2026` (3.7+, Jun 10 2026).
- **Root cause**: `cursor.com/changelog` is a paginated feed, not an archive, and Cursor's release cadence is high (3.6 → 3.8 in ~2 weeks). Fetching the listing root and finding nothing is therefore a *pagination artifact*, not evidence of absence — but it reads identically to one, which is what pushed the implement run into leaning on someone else's verification instead of its own.
- **Apply next time**: never treat a miss on `cursor.com/changelog` (or any paginated vendor feed) as a verification result in either direction. Resolve the dated permalink first — WebSearch the feature name plus version, or try `cursor.com/changelog/<MM-DD-YY>` / the feature slug — then WebFetch that permalink and quote it verbatim. If the permalink cannot be reached, say the claim is UNVERIFIED explicitly; do not ship it on a third party's verification trail while implying the build checked it. Generalizes beyond Cursor: for any external-claim check, "the index page didn't show it" is never the same as "it isn't there".

## L-2026-07-24-14 — A new git-range scope in a skill must be derived from the repo's existing range convention, not authored fresh; two-dot `origin/<b>..HEAD` is the default wrong answer

- **Source**: eva-fix-pr run for ievo-ai/skills#447 (branch `eva-impl/445-deep-review-committed-diff-fallback`, cross-repo fix)
- **Signal**: Eva's REQUEST_CHANGES review blocked on `deep-review/SKILL.md`'s new committed-diff fallback using two-dot `origin/<default-branch>..HEAD`. For `git diff` that literally means `diff origin/<b> HEAD`, so on a branch merely *behind* the default branch, every default-branch-only commit renders as a reversed deletion and the deep-reviewer reports them as findings — the exact false-positive class the skill exists to avoid. The repo already had the correct form in a sibling file, `plugins/ievo/commands/vuln-scan.md:42`: `git diff "$(git merge-base HEAD "origin/$BASE_BRANCH")"..HEAD`, together with a three-tier default-branch cascade (`git symbolic-ref` → `gh repo view --json defaultBranchRef` → warn+`main`) that the new code also didn't reuse (it invented a `git remote show origin` fallback, a blocking network round-trip).
- **Root cause**: the implementer authored the range from first principles because the *issue* (#445) was framed as "the cascade never considers committed changes" — a control-flow gap — so the work was scoped to adding a tier, and the range expression inside that tier was treated as an incidental detail rather than a repo convention with an existing owner. The intervening self-review round (commit 595c892) reinforced the miss: it audited the two resolution paths for *ref-qualification consistency* (`main` vs `origin/main`) and fixed that, which made the block look reviewed while never questioning the `..` semantics or checking whether a sibling command already solved the same problem.
- **Apply next time**: before writing ANY git range, default-branch resolution, or diff-scope expression into an iEvo skill/command, `grep -rn "merge-base\|symbolic-ref refs/remotes/origin/HEAD\|defaultBranchRef" plugins/ievo/` and copy the existing form verbatim — `commands/vuln-scan.md`'s `--diff` scope block is the canonical one. Prefer three-dot/`merge-base` over two-dot in every diff-against-a-base-branch context; reserve two-dot for a range the user typed explicitly. And when a self-review round touches a code block, re-derive the block's whole contract (what range? what semantics?) rather than only closing the one inconsistency that caught the eye — fixing an internal inconsistency inside a wrong expression makes the expression look vetted.

## L-2026-07-24-15 — A "matches <sibling file>" parity claim written to justify a fix is asserted, never diffed; and a CHANGELOG entry's **Version** bullet is a required structural element, not optional prose

- **Source**: eva-fix-pr run for ievo-ai/skills#447 (branch `eva-impl/445-deep-review-committed-diff-fallback`, cross-repo fix, round 2)
- **Signal**: Eva's REQUEST_CHANGES on round 1's own output raised two findings, both in prose rather than behaviour ("Fix itself is correct"). (1) The `## v0.61.1` CHANGELOG section carried no `**Version**` bullet, though every entry v0.60.2→v0.61.0 has one — leaving `**Scope** — single-file change` reading as a description of the whole diff when `git diff --name-only origin/main...HEAD` shows **8** files (the 6 mandatory version-bump files + CHANGELOG + the one substantive skill file). Since `cut-release.yml` copies the section verbatim into the public Release body, the misstatement ships to users. (2) Both the CHANGELOG Fix bullet and `deep-review/SKILL.md:77` claimed the new default-branch resolution was "the same resolution cascade … `commands/vuln-scan.md` uses" — false: `commands/vuln-scan.md:33-40` has a **third** tier (`BASE_BRANCH="main"` + warning) that deep-review deliberately does not carry, exiting instead.
- **Root cause**: both defects were *introduced by the previous fix round* while correcting a real bug. Round 1 adopted `vuln-scan.md`'s merge-base form per `[[L-2026-07-24-14]]` and wrote "matches vuln-scan.md" as the **justification** for doing so — a parity claim authored to argue for a change is asserted from the reason it was made, not read back tier-by-tier against the file it names, so the one tier that intentionally diverged went unrecorded and the doc became a false spec. The missing `**Version**` bullet has the same shape: the version bump itself was done correctly in all six files, so the *work* was complete and only its changelog bullet was absent — and nothing catches that, because AGENTS.md § changelog structure states the required skeleton in prose ("reflow the same rationale, coverage/version-bump, and validator notes into bullets") while no pre-commit hook or CI validator lints CHANGELOG section structure. A fix round edits one bullet in place and never re-reads the section against the template.
- **Apply next time**: (a) treat every "matches / mirrors / same as `<other file>`" claim as a **verifiable assertion with an owner** — open the named file at the moment of writing, diff it clause-by-clause against the new code, and state any deliberate divergence *plus its reason* inline; a parity claim that omits a divergence is worse than no claim, because the next reader takes it as the contract. (b) After any CHANGELOG edit on a version-bumping PR, re-read the new `## vX.Y.Z` section against the two entries above it as a template — `**Version**` and the `---` separator are structural, and a `**Scope**` bullet claiming "single-file change" must be reconciled against `git diff --name-only origin/<base>...HEAD` before it ships (the count includes the mechanical bump files, so the honest phrasings are "one substantive file" + a `**Version**` bullet accounting for the rest). (c) Generalises past this repo: when a review round rewrites a code block, re-derive the *prose describing that block* in the same pass — round 1 here fixed the range semantics and left two sentences describing the old, wrong understanding.
- Related: `[[L-2026-07-24-14]]` (same PR, round 1 — derive a git range from the repo's existing convention), `[[L-2026-07-24-10]]` (a PR body's own "found and fixed" claim trusted instead of re-verified), `[[L-2026-07-23-04]]` (why `/ievo:evolution` was **not** invoked for this capture — `$TARGET_REPO` is `ievo-ai/skills`, the skill's own source repo, so its marker-injection write path would be out-of-scope file churn; appended to `EVA_EVOLUTION_CAPTURE` directly instead).

## L-2026-07-24-16 — Third observed recurrence of the wrong `origin` remote on a cross-repo eva-fix-pr run; the prompt's "do NOT change git remotes" rule still has no carve-out

- **Source**: eva-fix-pr run for ievo-ai/skills#447 (cross-repo fix, round 2)
- **Signal**: exactly as recorded in `[[L-2026-07-24-11]]` and `[[L-2026-07-15-01]]` — `git remote get-url origin` resolved to `…/ievo-ai/eva.git` while the working tree was genuinely `ievo-ai/skills`' PR head (local `HEAD` == the PR's `head.sha`, and `origin/main` == `ievo-ai/skills`' `main`). Proof that it is the URL and not the content: `gh api repos/ievo-ai/eva/commits/<head.sha>` returned HTTP 422 "No commit found", while the same call against `ievo-ai/skills` resolved. `git ls-remote origin <head-branch>` returned nothing. The prompt's literal `git push origin HEAD` would have created a foreign branch in `ievo-ai/eva`, and a `git fetch origin main` executed *before* correcting the URL would have repointed `refs/remotes/origin/main` at eva's unrelated `main` and inflated the Phase 2 `[pr-fix-N]` budget count.
- **Root cause**: unchanged and still undiagnosed at the harness level; the working directory's *content* is swapped to the cross-repo target but `origin`'s URL is left pointing at the runner's nominal repo (eva) — most plausibly the `claude-code-action` git-credential setup, which configures the remote for `github.repository` rather than the checked-out `repository:` input. The prompt-level blocker is also unchanged: the `eva-fix-pr` auth preamble states "Do NOT change tokens or git remotes" as non-negotiable, which read literally forbids the exact remediation this store prescribes.
- **Apply next time**: keep applying `[[L-2026-07-24-11]]`'s remedy — verify the remote against `$TARGET_REPO` **before** the Phase 2 budget fetch, repoint with the SAME token (`git remote set-url origin` at `$TARGET_REPO`, reusing the token already embedded in the existing URL so no principal changes), and say so in the round's PR comment. Third recurrence in ~10 days across both eva-implement and eva-fix-pr means the store entry is *working* but is a per-run manual step on a critical path: escalate to the operator to (i) add an explicit carve-out sentence to the eva-fix-pr prompt's safety rules, and (ii) add a deterministic pre-agent workflow step that asserts `git remote get-url origin` contains `$TARGET_REPO` and repoints it if not — a shell guard cannot forget, and it removes the rule/store contradiction entirely.

## L-2026-07-25-01 — A length-capped field's pre-edit headroom is not permission to append; re-measure the edited value before the gate

- **Source**: eva-implement run for ievo-ai/skills#432 (branch `eva-impl/432-codex-client-gating`, cross-repo build)
- **Signal**: first `pre-commit run --all-files` failed with `compatibility-too-long: compatibility is 526 chars — exceeds agentskills.io spec limit of 500` on `evo-auto-disable/SKILL.md` — the ONE compatibility field of the four touched that I had appended to instead of rewriting.
- **Root cause**: I applied `[[L-2026-07-24-12]]`'s "an existing field near the cap must be rewritten, not appended to" by measuring all four fields up front (492/486/481/432) and sorting them into "near cap → rewrite carefully" (three) vs "has headroom → safe to append" (evo-auto-disable at 432). The 94-char append blew the cap anyway. The pre-edit measurement answered "which fields are risky?" when the gate's actual question is "is the POST-edit value ≤ 500?" — a question only measuring the edited result can answer, and I never re-measured before running the gate.
- **Apply next time**: when editing any value with a hard length cap (SKILL.md `compatibility:` ≤500, `description` ≤1024, or any schema-capped field), compute the new value's length as part of writing it — before the gate run, for every edited field, regardless of how much headroom the old value had. Pre-edit triage ("near cap" vs "headroom") only decides how aggressively to trim; it never exempts a field from the post-edit measurement.


## L-2026-07-25-02 — A client-path gating fix must sweep every lifecycle surface touching the vendor dir, not just the components the issue names
- **Source**: eva-fix-pr run for ievo-ai/skills#454
- **Signal**: Eva's review on #454 found `/ievo:evo` (SKILL.md target scan + vendor writes), `/ievo:update` (local-copy reads/overwrites), and `/ievo:uninstall` (marker scan) still hardwired to `.claude/skills/` after the PR gated `init` + the evo-auto toggles on `$CODEX_CLI` — on Codex, the first `/ievo:evo` re-vendor would recreate the exact stranded-copy bug (#432) the init fix had just repaired, and init's new Codex summary advertised `/ievo:update`, which could not see `.agents/skills/`.
- **Root cause**: the implementer scoped the sweep to the components issue #432 named (init, evo-auto) instead of deriving the sweep set from the invariant being changed — "every component that reads or writes the client vendor path". A `grep -rln '\.claude/skills' plugins/ievo/` would have enumerated the true component set (including `commands/update.md`/`commands/uninstall.md`, a directory the issue never mentioned) in seconds. Same failure class as [[L-2026-07-24-06]]: sweep defined by the issue's list, not by a criterion applied to the filesystem.
- **Apply next time**: when a change alters a path, convention, or invariant, derive the affected set by grepping the repo for the OLD invariant's literal (path fragment, field name, event name) and fix or explicitly justify every hit — the issue text locates the symptom, never the blast radius. Also cross-check every user-facing advert the change adds ("run /X later") against whether /X actually works under the new convention; an advert pointing at an ungated sibling is itself a finding.


## L-2026-07-25-03 — A skill with a delegated sub-agent twin duplicates its logic; gating the skill without mirroring the twin leaves the primary dispatch path unfixed
- **Source**: eva-fix-pr run for ievo-ai/skills#454 (round 2)
- **Signal**: Eva's re-review on #454 found `agents/evolution.md` — the sub-agent `/ievo:evo` delegates to FIRST when available — still scanning `.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md` and vendoring to `.claude/skills/<name>/`, reinstating #432 on the primary evo dispatch path even though `evo/SKILL.md` itself was client-gated in round 1. The twin WAS present in the round-1 blast-radius grep (`grep -rln '\.claude/skills' plugins/ievo/` lists `agents/evolution.md`), but the fix set was assembled from the lifecycle commands/skills the review named, so the `agents/` hit was never opened. Same for the `handoff`/`extract-best-practices` inventories and `consolidate`'s package-registration write path.
- **Root cause**: iEvo pairs several skills with a delegated sub-agent twin that duplicates the skill's steps in a separate file (`evo/SKILL.md` ↔ `agents/evolution.md`; same pattern for `security-check` ↔ `security-auditor.md`, `vuln-scan` ↔ `vuln-scanner.md`, `deep-review` ↔ `deep-reviewer.md`). A behavioral fix applied to the skill body silently misses the twin — and the twin is what actually runs whenever the sub-agent is available; the skill's inline steps are only the fallback. The blast-radius grep was run but its hits were triaged by directory ("commands/ and skills/ are the lifecycle surfaces") instead of opening every hit.
- **Apply next time**: in `ievo-ai/skills`, whenever changing behavior in a skill whose body has an "if the <X> sub-agent is available, delegate" preamble, treat `plugins/ievo/agents/<twin>.md` as a mandatory second edit site and diff the two step sequences for parity before committing. Generally: a blast-radius grep ([[L-2026-07-25-02]]) only closes the class if EVERY hit is opened or explicitly justified in the PR text — a hit skipped because its directory "belongs to another concern" is exactly where the next review round lands.


## L-2026-07-25-04 — `git status --porcelain` hides gitignored paths; use `git check-ignore`/`git ls-files` to assert a path is excluded, not a `??` match

- **Source**: eva-implement run for ievo-ai/skills#446 (branch `eva-impl/446-evo-auto-hooks-gitignore-shim`)
- **Signal**: first `node --test` run of the new `evo-auto-hooks-lifecycle.test.mjs` regression test failed on `the *.local.sh companion stays untracked (never accidentally committed)` — the assertion expected `git status --porcelain` in the clone to contain a `?? .ievo/hooks/scripts/correction-capture.local.sh` line, but the command's actual output was the empty string.
- **Root cause**: the test wrote `correction-capture.local.sh` at a path the fix's own new gitignore negation pattern (`.ievo/hooks/scripts/*` minus the three tracked shim names) correctly excludes — so it's not merely untracked, it's *ignored*, and `git status --porcelain` omits ignored paths entirely unless `--ignored` is passed. I wrote the assertion assuming "untracked" and "ignored" surface the same way in plain `git status` output; they don't — `??` only appears for untracked-but-not-ignored paths.
- **Apply next time**: to assert a path is excluded from commits by design (gitignored), assert with `git check-ignore -q <path>` (exit 0) and/or confirm its absence from `git ls-files`, not with `git status --porcelain`'s default `??` marker — that marker is reserved for untracked-and-not-ignored paths and silently returns nothing for the ignored case, so a test relying on it will report false negatives (empty status) rather than a clear mismatch. If asserting via `git status`, pass `--ignored` explicitly and match the `!!` prefix instead.

## L-2026-07-25-05 — A `# shellcheck disable=CODE` directive cannot carry trailing prose on the same line; and its scope is "the next statement" (all its continuation lines), not the whole run: block

- **Source**: eva-implement run for ievo-ai/eva#259 (branch `eva-impl/actionlint-ci-gate`)
